<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Simply the functional purpose of each of the things you're trying to do. ## assistant

**The two-device work means this:** a student starts a test on a laptop, then continues on a phone. The phone takes control. The laptop must stop saving so it cannot overwrite the phone’s answers.

**That is one of the most time-consuming checks. It is a reasonable candidate to defer if switching devices during an unfinished test is not important right now.** Normal save, reload and retry still matter.

Below is my ranking from **most impactful to least impactful for the current release**. The times are rough estimates for finishing and checking each item. Some checks overlap, so don’t add every row together.

| Rank | Thing we would finish | Its plain functional purpose | Time |
|---:|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Student ownership and teacher permissions** | A student sees and changes only their own work. You can help the selected student without accidentally changing someone else’s records. | **4–8 hours** |
| **2** | **Normal save failure and retry** | If the internet fails, the test stops safely. Answers stay available. Retry saves them before the student continues. Much is already built; remaining cases need checking. | **3–6 hours** |
| **3** | **Assignment completion across all views** | A student finishes homework. My Work, Home and your teacher page all show the same finished assignment. Two copies of the same homework stay separate. | **4–7 hours** |
| **4** | **Correct Progress numbers** | The score uses the right answers and counts. Repeating a question does not pretend the student got it right the first time. Your view matches theirs. | **3–5 hours** |
| **5** | **LR drill mode and source saving** | A saved drill remembers whether it was training or a test, and which questions it used. Those facts survive closing and reopening it. | **2–4 hours** |
| **6** | **Review behavior** | A student can revisit the exact missed question, explain the mistake and finish the review. Their original answer and flag remain saved. Missing it again creates new review work. | **3–6 hours** |
| **7** | **Library exercise resume and finish** | A student leaves an Activity or bootcamp, returns to the same place, then finishes it. The app records the finish correctly. | **3–5 hours** |
| **8** | **Teacher controls and answer separation** | You can assign, inspect, leave notes and select review work. Answering a practice question yourself never changes the student’s score. | **3–6 hours** |
| **9** | **Home’s next action** | After a student finishes something, Home points them to the right next task. Your assigned priority stays ahead of an automatic suggestion. | **1–3 hours** |
| **10** | **Navigation and smaller screens** | Old links still work. Students can find everything and read questions on a phone, laptop or narrow window. Locked pages stay locked. | **3–5 hours** |
| **11** | **Session prep, recap and follow-up** | A student opens their appointment and finds what to do before it, what happened during it and what to do afterward. You see the same items. | **4–7 hours**, including deployment |
| **12** | **Weekly plan publishing** | You publish a week of work. The student sees that exact plan, its progress and how each task finishes. A failed publish can be retried safely. | **2–4 hours** |
| **13** | **Study settings** | A student changes timing or feedback settings and understands what will happen. New work follows the setting; old scores stay unchanged. | **2–4 hours** |
| **14** | **Exam result details** | After a test, the student can expand the result and see each question’s answer, time, notes, flags and review link. | **2–4 hours** |
| **15** | **Save succeeded, but the reply disappeared** | The server saved the answer, but the browser never heard back. Retry must find that saved answer rather than create a second one. | **3–6 hours** |
| **16** | **Access removed during a test** | If access is removed while a test is open, the student keeps their old results but cannot continue saving newly restricted work. | **2–4 hours** |
| **17** | **Two-device switching** | Move an unfinished test from laptop to phone. Only the device now in control can save. | **4–8 hours** |
| **18** | **Remaining study-tool checks** | Bookmarks, highlighting, finding words and crossing out choices keep working after navigation or reload without changing the saved answer. Most tools already shipped. | **1–3 hours** |
| **19** | **Extra written explanations** | A student can read a second explanation you provide, clearly labeled as yours. | **1–3 hours**, excluding writing explanations |
| **20** | **Final design review** | Find confusing layouts, hard-to-read text and difficult controls across the finished app, then fix them. You already deferred this. | **6–10 hours** |

**Most time:** the final design review as a whole; two-device switching among individual technical tasks; assignment consistency and Schedule are also substantial.

**Least time:** checking Home’s next action, finishing study-tool checks, and adding the optional second-explanation display.

**My strongest keep recommendation is ranks 1–8.** They protect answers, homework, scores and your ability to teach. **Two-device switching, extra explanations and the final design pass are the clearest deferral candidates.** Deferring two-device acceptance would mean we do not claim that switching devices during an unfinished test is fully verified.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 6.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"741176","wall_time_seconds":2.6026953,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2194,"output":"# AGENTS.md\\n\\nThis file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.\\n\\n## Fast Locator\\n\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\n- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`\\n- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`\\n\\nIf an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\n\\n## Project Map\\n\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\n\\n## Stack And Commands\\n\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\n\\n## Working Rules\\n\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\n\\n## Product Constraints\\n\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\n\\n## Smart Drill Note\\n\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\n\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\n\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\n\\n## Workflow Expectations\\n\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\n\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\n\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\n\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\n\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\n\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\n\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\n\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\n\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\n\\n## Known Gaps\\n\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\n\\n## Agent skills\\n\\n### Issue tracker\\n\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\n\\n### Triage labels\\n\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\n\\n### Domain docs\\n\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"2f90b2","wall_time_seconds":2.6832028,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":11,"output":"abd65fcee1ccdba399154c2833eb07499958c3a5\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"782114","wall_time_seconds":0.8043027,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4163,"output":"# Ship the atomic PrepTest writer before standalone timed work\\n\\nDate: 2026-09-30\\n\\nStatus: **Inactive PrepTest control, typed writer, child enforcement, server manifest, and local SQL verification artifacts are prepared. No backend deployment or switch of existing frontend writers.** See [writer readiness and limits](2026-09-30-timed-pt-writer-readiness.md) and [the earlier foundation phase](2026-09-30-timed-pt-foundation-readiness.md). Ticket #167 remains open. This expands [the proposed timed-write contract](2026-09-30-timed-write-contract.md); that contract\'s live metadata evidence and deployment approval requirements still apply.\\n\\nThe first backend release should cover PrepTest parent state, LR legs, RC legs, and the break together. Standalone timed LR/RC follows as a separate adoption phase. A parent-only RPC would leave the child-write race intact.\\n\\n## The existing boundaries determine the first batch\\n\\n| Existing boundary | Current behavior | Required v1 replacement |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| `src/lib/practiceTestStore.ts` | Parent creation, revision CAS, device claim, pending device copies | Versioned create/claim commands and authoritative command responses; existing reads and backup ownership checks remain |\\n| `src/lib/drillResumeSession.ts` | Separate parent-device check, then official LR KV snapshot upsert | LR snapshot checkpoint under the parent lock; retained device copy until acknowledgement |\\n| `src/pages/Drill.tsx`, `src/lib/sectionHistorySave.ts` | LR closeout writes attempts, WAJ, history, resume completion in separate requests | One official close-leg command; explicit projection completion contract |\\n| `src/features/rc/api/rcProgress.ts` | Session creation, answer insert, clock/position update, and completion each check/write separately | Typed RC start/checkpoint/close-leg commands |\\n| `src/features/rc/pages/RcPlayer.tsx` | Questions and clock gated on account reads/saves; closeout writes blank first-attempt rows | Consume acknowledged session/position/clock; closeout batch and parent advance commit together |\\n| `src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx` | Reads child results and later saves parent advancement/break/final score | Read authoritative parent returned by close-leg; parent and break commands cannot accept arbitrary JSON patches |\\n| `src/lib/practiceTestRunner.ts`, `src/lib/practiceTestReview.ts` | Read attributed historical LR/RC records | Preserve result/review reads; v1 completion is not inferred from a child row that has not committed parent advancement |\\n\\nRepository schema is concrete but is not a fresh live DDL verification. `practice_test_runs.id` is **text**, not UUID. Its statuses are `in-progress`, `complete`, `abandoned`; RC uses `in_progress`, `completed`, `abandoned`. LR official records are JSON in `bootcamp_kv`, under `practice_sessions`; do not assume a normalized LR sessions table exists. Keep the installed owner FKs and per-leg/first-attempt unique indexes described in the contract.\\n\\n## Phase 0: freeze behavior and reproduce the races\\n\\nPrepare an ordinary-authenticated two-client harness against an isolated database with the installed schema. Refresh the live metadata inventory before deployment. Exercise existing parent CAS, then demonstrate that separate child preflight/writes can race takeover. No service-role success is permission evidence.\\n\\nRecord these current semantics before implementation:\\n\\n- Parent `section_seconds` freezes the user\'s chosen timing. The break is 600 seconds. Historical null timing retains existing reader compatibility.\\n- LR permits overtime and records `regulation_answered_qids`. Preserve that distinction; do not silently introduce automatic submission at budget expiry.\\n- RC answer column is `selected`; null closeout rows count as unanswered, and session elapsed time is not the sum of per-question times.\\n- Current scoring is client-derived. This work protects transactional ownership, clock state, and acknowledged results. It does **not** introduce a new secure-grade product requirement or claim server-verified answer correctness. Validate result structure and derive parent totals from acknowledged child results. Preserve existing scoring formulas and explicitly retain their trust boundary.\\n- Current LR closeout requires its expected WAJ work before the leg is considered finished. Do not move it after advancement without a separately recorded change to that behavior.\\n\\n## Phase 1: add protected control state and receipts\\n\\nPrepare two forward migrations, not a historical-record rewrite:\\n\\n1. `supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_timed_work_v1_control.sql`: control fields, receipt table, typed RPC helpers, and owner result reads.\\n2. `supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_timed_work_v1_enforcement.sql`: v1 direct-write restrictions and exhaustive OLD/NEW attribution protection.\\n\\nThe exact timestamps are assigned when implementation starts. Keep deployment of enforcement and adapter activation coordinated; an RPC left beside unrestricted protected raw writes does not provide the guarantee.\\n\\nFor the PT phase add nullable `timed_contract_version` to `practice_test_runs`, plus server-owned writer epoch, phase, accumulated elapsed milliseconds, running anchor, lease expiry, and active clock leg. Keep existing `revision` as the single command CAS version. Preserve existing device ID for UI display; possession of an arbitrary device ID alone does not authorize a write. Epoch/lease/revision are validated under the parent lock.\\n\\nConstraints for protected rows:\\n\\n- Version is supported; revision/epoch/elapsed are nonnegative.\\n- Running phases have an anchor and lease; paused/terminal phases have no running anchor.\\n- Clock leg matches the current frozen leg, or the phase is the one permitted break.\\n- Owner, PT, pace, section duration, frozen leg definition, and protected version cannot be changed by a client patch.\\n- Terminal state cannot reopen. No protected source or completion record is deleted by this API.\\n\\nUse a companion binding table for LR KV records. Its owner/run/leg, namespace, record ID, and frozen queue identity are immutable, and there is one binding per PT LR leg. The existing key `drill::practice-test::<runId>::leg-<index>` and `practiceSessionIdFor` can be preserved for PT compatibility. A generic owner JSON blob must not become writer/access authority.\\n\\nUse a durable command receipt table with unique `(owner_id, work_kind, work_id, command_id)`, canonical payload hash, committed revision, and committed result. A command UUID is separate from an RC attempt UUID. Store receipts in the same transaction as progress. Authenticated clients can read their own receipts but cannot insert/update/delete them directly. Retain receipts long enough for supported recovery; a TTL policy is not part of this batch.\\n\\n## Phase 2: implement one typed PT transaction\\n\\nPublic entry points: `create_timed_practice_test_v1` and `commit_timed_work_v1`. Creation cannot take an arbitrary owner. New protected children are created inside start-leg, not by direct browser inserts.\\n\\nThe commit envelope is `{workKind, workId, expectedRevision, writerEpoch, deviceId, commandId, operation, payload}`. Derive owner from `auth.uid()`. Parse only the fields for the named operation and reject unknown/immutable patches. Start with PT operations: claim/adopt, start-leg, checkpoint, pause, resume, close-leg, start-break, finish-break, abandon. Final completion is derived when the final leg closes; there is no unrestricted set-score or set-current-leg operation.\\n\\nEach successful command performs these steps:\\n\\n1. Resolve the actor-owned parent and lock it `FOR UPDATE`. Look for the command receipt under that lock. A matching receipt acknowledges the old committed operation before stale-revision or current-access rejection; changed payload rejects. Return both the receipt and **current** authoritative parent so retrying an old receipt cannot restore an older UI state.\\n2. Validate supported version, expected revision, writer epoch, lease, exact phase/leg, and immutable child binding. A new stale command returns a typed conflict with current state and makes no progress writes.\\n3. Check current PT entitlement using the existing `can_sit_practice_test` rules. Lock the actual grant row used for authorization: [REDACTED] profile feature row, exact owned/unlocked/nonarchived assignment, or admin-role row. A boolean precheck alone does not serialize a concurrent revoke. Use deterministic lock order and test it against the revocation paths. Missing access rejects new writes while preserving history and receipts.\\n4. Settle clock time using server time, bounded by the acknowledged lease expiry, then apply the operation. Never decrease elapsed time. Heartbeat renews only a valid active writer. Proposed 15-second heartbeat/45-second lease remains a proposal to verify, not an adopted product constant.\\n5. Persist LR snapshot or RC answer batch together with elapsed time and position. Validate qid membership, queue order, question count, selected letters/nulls, index bounds, and exact parent/leg/session attribution. Do not regenerate missing content or accept a new queue during recovery. Keep all acknowledged choices through takeover.\\n6. On close-leg, persist the official child result, required closeout projections, child terminal state, parent leg result, and advance/break/final state in one transaction. Increment parent revision once, write receipt, and return parent plus child checkpoint and server time.\\n\\nRC retains existing first-attempt uniqueness. Same attempt UUID with the same owner/session/question/passage/choice is idempotent; different content rejects. A different first-attempt UUID for an already committed question conflicts rather than replacing the original. Review reanswers require their separate allowed semantics and cannot reopen an exam leg. Clock checkpoint and answer acknowledgement must share the command; current split calls are not enough.\\n\\nLR stores the frozen `PracticeSession` shape and validates stable attribution outside that JSON. Do not trust supplied `user_id`, `pool_slug`, `completion_id`, or arbitrary section-history parent links. Keep unanswered/answered/overtime distinctions and current score trust. Validate totals against the frozen queue and derive parent aggregation from the acknowledged child record rather than a later unrelated readback.\\n\\nFor LR WAJ/analytics, inventory `commitSessionAttempts`, `logSectionWrongAnswers`, and `persistSectionResults` before moving their writes. Either put mandatory rows in the transaction, or introduce an explicit closeout-pending phase with an idempotent transactionally enqueued projection job. In the latter design, parent advancement still waits for required projection acknowledgement. Mail remains optional and cannot block the official leg, matching current behavior. Do not leave the existing client closeout path writing official rows around the RPC.\\n\\n## Phase 3: close direct-write bypasses before switching clients\\n\\nProtected parent INSERT/UPDATE, attributed LR KV INSERT/UPDATE, RC session/attempt INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE, and official section-result INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE must reject ordinary authenticated direct mutations. Restrict metadata-only RC operations deliberately rather than accidentally disabling exclusions/review flags. Preserve ordinary bootcamp data and historical reads.\\n\\nEnforcement checks **OLD and NEW** protection, owner, source identity, and attribution. Clearing the PT FK, setting contract version null, moving a protected KV record, or inserting an alternate child for the same protected leg cannot escape it. Reserved PT LR source keys cannot be recreated through generic KV writes. Replace conflicting permissive policies; adding a stricter permissive policy leaves the existing owner policy effective.\\n\\nPrefer a dedicated tightly privileged no-login executor role for protected RPCs if Lovable\'s managed migration role supports that ownership setup. Authenticated/anonymous must have no membership in it. Security-definer functions fix search path, qualify tables, check `auth.uid()`, and receive only narrow grants. Ordinary callers must not be able to forge an RPC-only bypass with a custom setting. Verify managed-role capabilities before selecting an enforcement technique; do not equate every `postgres`-owned definer function with this authorized writer.\\n\\n## Phase 4: switch PT adapters and explicitly adopt active work\\n\\nAdd `src/lib/timedWorkClient.ts` for typed command envelopes/responses and conflict/access/expired-writer errors. Backups retain the same pending command UUID, payload, expected revision, and epoch until acknowledged. Never mint a fresh command on a lost-response retry.\\n\\nSwitch the existing boundaries in the first table. The parent returned by close-leg drives advancement; `PracticeTestRun.tsx` stops performing a second parent save for that v1 leg. `RcPlayer.tsx` and `Drill.tsx` stop clocks and hide/gate questions on loss of authoritative writer state until claim/recovery is acknowledged. Break pause/resume/finish uses the same contract. Device elapsed time is recovery evidence, not server proof of an instantaneous offline pause.\\n\\nNew PTs use v1 only after backend readiness is verified. Existing active rows enter through explicit adoption/claim under the parent lock. Validate and preserve owner, current leg, frozen queue/passages, acknowledged answers, saved position, timing, and terminal results. Adopt only a known server-owned child; unknown or contradictory device-only copies need explicit recovery rather than automatic import. Already completed/abandoned rows remain readable and are not rewritten.\\n\\nAn old browser meeting an adopted record must fail its raw writes and require refresh. Compatibility tests must include bypass attempts through every legacy writer, not only the new adapter\'s happy path.\\n\\n## Phase 5: give standalone LR/RC durable sitting identities\\n\\nDo not include this in the initial PT rollout. Standalone LR currently uses `drill::section::ptX-sY` with a deterministic record ID; retakes overwrite that identity. A control row keyed only to it would confuse terminal history with a new sitting.\\n\\nIntroduce a distinct v1 sitting UUID and immutable control/source identity. Use a distinct v1 LR KV record per sitting plus a current-active lookup, or normalized canonical snapshot storage. A unique active `(owner, source_kind, source_key)` permits one active writer while retaining completed retakes. RC already has a session UUID, but it still needs frozen standalone source authorization and control binding.\\n\\nInventory every supported standalone source before enforcement: feature-granted manual full section, exact classroom/homework assignment, bootcamp source, and any supported custom timed source. Freeze source identity and check its current ownership/unlock/archive state on every command. Unsupported source kinds fail explicitly. Broad access can authorize its supported source kinds; an unrelated assignment cannot authorize an arbitrary queue. Retain existing timing/retake behavior and clearly identify any temporarily excluded legacy paths.\\n\\n## Code prepared is separate from deployed and verified\\n\\nImplementation deliverables before approval:\\n\\n- Reviewed additive schema/RPC and enforcement migrations, with immutable/terminal/owner constraints and a rollback-free forward recovery plan.\\n- An isolated PostgreSQL transaction harness covering same-revision races, save-versus-takeover, save-versus-revoke, changed-payload replay, old-receipt replay after later work, lease expiry, failed closeout, and raw OLD/NEW attribution bypasses.\\n- Frontend adapter tests for pending command retention, authoritative response consumption, no duplicate advancement, older clients, adoption, failed hydration, and frozen queues.\\n- Typecheck/build and student/teacher browser evidence before frontend release.\\n\\nRefresh live metadata immediately before deployment. Joshua must confirm the Lovable Cloud backend route and immediately approve any credit-using action. No Supabase CLI Edge Function deployment under the current managed backend. Generate database types only from the deployed schema; temporary typed RPC boundaries may be isolated in the new client until regeneration, not hand edits to generated types.\\n\\nReport four separate states: **code prepared**, **backend deployed**, **frontend switched**, **live acceptance verified**. Run ordinary authenticated two-device/outage/revocation acceptance for PT LR, PT RC, and break before expanding rollout. Later run standalone LR/RC separately. Keep #167 open while any supported active path lacks the contract or its acceptance evidence.\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ccbb32","wall_time_seconds":0.560869,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":817,"output":"# Progress evidence and History prepared in isolation\\n\\nBaseline 72df6964df9177bc5971cac9fdb4512d0b545a17. Branch codex/progress-173. This slice is not published and does not close #173.\\n\\nImplemented a shared-ledger detailed History table with URL-backed area, result, first/repeat, source, mode, persisted type, record/question search and order. Filtering never promotes repeats to first answers. Non-date sorts use the table; returning to date grouping resets a non-date order. Stale URL options remain visibly selected and can be cleared. Existing date-grouped view remains available. Unsupported blind-answer/timing/flag/note/assignment/taxonomy fields are explicitly disclosed rather than invented.\\n\\nAccuracy and topic percentages are withheld independently for affected LR/RC sources when earlier first answers may be missing. Candidate counts and evidence IDs remain available with an explicit warning; these are not complete first-ever accuracy. Topic denominators now expose their exact record IDs. History labels partial first answers as First available answer. Selected-student dossier Accuracy, History and Topics share subject and URL window; one control is shown and does not overwrite the teacher\'s own saved practice window. Teacher actions return to the selected dossier rather than own practice.\\n\\nHost verification passed 40 tests across 12 focused files, including first/window/repeat/source eligibility, filtering, partial LR/parent-source cases, table URLs, stale filters, nested teacher links, topic evidence, dossier composition and selected-subject window storage. The affected History/accuracy seven-test suite passed again after the final URL-mode fix. Production build and canonical-content scan passed for the final source. Build-generated bank/finder/MCP churn was restored only in this owned checkout.\\n\\nIndependent Codex agent review found sort/partial-copy/route-test issues that were corrected. Opus 5.5 medium inspected the final source in claudex-xsx4zcqw and returned APPROVED with low findings. Earlier inspection claudex-knhiot_v was invalidated by concurrent build-generated changes and is not an approval. Intermediate claudex-h0wntsd9 approved a prior snapshot; the final snapshot supersedes it.\\n\\nLow findings retained for later acceptance: an RC-session-only History regression can strengthen coverage. Question ordering now uses numeric collation so Q2 precedes Q10; four helper tests, typecheck and the final production build passed. Independent Opus 5.5 medium inspection claudex-674r875g approved this follow-up. Candidate correct/total counts remain intentionally visible for auditability while withheld percentages and first-available labels disclose uncertainty. Joshua is the prescribed-priority authority in the current product, so named copy is retained. No live sample reconciliation or browser-responsive acceptance was performed. The previously blocked browser pane was not accessed through a workaround. Browser Back/reload preserves URL filters; no explicit Review return-state contract is claimed.\\n\\nNo database, saved-attempt, grading-adapter or AI changes. Claude\'s weekly/settings implementation is in a separate checkout; no overlapping files or integration commit exists yet. Final host typecheck passed.\\n"}'}]

Dedicated `/testing/test/:runId/lr/:legIndex` and `/testing/test/:runId/rc/:legIndex` routes render `TimedOfficialChildRunner` through `timedChildController`. Existing Drill, RC player, legacy session stores and section-history writers do not mount in this branch. Completed legacy report routes remain separate. The foundation, full writer and metadata upgrade are now deployed; the 59-row catalog is seeded and configuration is enabled with a 30,000ms lease. Code integration and isolated tests do not establish live acceptance evidence. #167 remains open until the rollout and live checks are complete.\\n\\nLR composes `TimedPracticeTestStore`. It restores the exact attributed `PracticeSession` queue, position, first answers, flags, switch count and regulation answer IDs. Before creating a checkpoint or close command it verifies canonical source identity and SHA-256 fingerprints against the immutable parent manifest. It imports the existing pure `normalizeQuestionTextFields` function, now exported with no behavior change. Shared passages, prompt repair and prose normalization therefore use the actual QuestionBank rules. Optional loaded QuestionBank rows must agree with the resulting normalized fields and ordered queue. Canonical input must include the entire source section, including removed positions needed by the normalizer; excluded positions cannot enter the scored queue.\\n\\nLR checkpoint commands carry typed selected letters, correctness, milliseconds, flags, switch counts, current position and optional regulation IDs. Close commands require the entire ordered matching answer-key set and an analysis object. Prepared commands are durable before transport; retries retain command ID, revision, epoch and the exact payload. Analysis and client-derived correctness retain the scoring trust boundary documented in the foundation plan. The catalog does not provide a new server-secret answer key.\\n\\nProtected `question_states` retain spent time, flags and eliminations even for an unanswered LR question, without inventing an answer attempt. The official route checkpoints picks/navigation immediately and renews through checkpoints every five seconds. It freezes regulation answer IDs at the cutoff, requires account acknowledgment, and then retains the existing LR overtime mode until explicit Finish. A failed read/save replaces questions with a paused recovery state; an existing pending command can only be retried as the same envelope. Clock estimates remain bounded by the acknowledged server lease. Async generation and owner/run/leg checks prevent delayed prior-route replies from changing another account\'s view or navigating it.\\n\\nRC composes `TimedWorkClient`. It restores the exact native question/passage attribution, session ownership and saved first-attempt UUIDs. It verifies passage and question text/choice fingerprints against the immutable RC manifest before exposing loaded content or preparing a command. It converts server child rows into the existing RC session and state shapes, deriving end-of-set reveal and section timing from protected parent state. It requires an explicit device identity; foreign writers, pending recovery and expired leases expose paused state. Preparation also checks the current monotonic sample age, preventing a once-valid view from creating commands after lease expiry. Running display time is capped at the account lease. A paused acknowledged child can close through the v1 command.\\n\\nRC revisits update the current choice under its stable first-attempt UUID; immutable command receipts preserve acknowledged revisions. Close analysis retains visit/answer-trail and personal marker telemetry in the account session configuration. Both child routes reuse the existing official question/passage panes, retain flags, eliminations, navigation, text size, find, highlights and pause/finish controls, and suppress answer/explanation reveal until the PrepTest returns to its completed report. Per-question dwell accrued while awaiting an acknowledgment is retained rather than overwritten by the older checkpoint payload.\\n\\nNeither adapter calls a legacy child or parent writer. Capability absence, permission denial, storage failure, malformed source, duplicate attribution or a lost receipt fail closed. A receipt whose parent has advanced to another leg returns no fabricated previous-child view. A future report integration must read the historical acknowledged child or official parent result separately; these adapters never infer a report from an unacknowledged local answer set.\\n\\nFocused tests cover real loader normalization without mutating source input, exact ordered content and SHA identity, owner/source/position/attempt mismatches, regulation attribution, full closeout keys, paused and expired clocks, foreign writers, storage failure, retained checkpoint/close command and RC attempt UUIDs after lost replies and reload. Controller tests exercise unanswered account metadata, stable-ID RC choice revisions and preserved telemetry through mock transactional acknowledgments. Rendered tests cover LR overtime, failed-save question suppression, retained-command retry, pending-ack dwell and account switching. A separate real-source parity suite validates all 177 offered LR/RC legs against the generated manifest and actual canonical JSON, using the real loader normalizer and mock versioned account reads. It pins PT118 removed question numbering. These isolated fixtures are not live backend evidence. The integrated parent/child suite passed 121 tests across 11 files. Root verified the existing legacy PT158 read-only view and unchanged saved data. Before claiming rollout acceptance, publish the frontend, verify fresh dedicated routes and historical reporting, and perform real lost-response, multi-device and revoked-access checks. Child implementation itself performed no production writes, backend activation or Lovable credit actions.\\n\\nCurrent rollout checkpoint: root temporarily disabled configuration after a live-connected local LR launch rejected valid PostgreSQL offset/microsecond timestamps. The narrow v1 LR adapter now uses strict explicit-zone ISO validation before normalizing the local parser input to UTC Z; invalid calendar, missing-zone and malformed timestamps still fail closed, and the authoritative account record is unchanged. Root re-enabled the same catalog and 30,000ms lease at 14:24 UTC. Actual LR browser verification then rendered the full question, acknowledged an answer and confirmed it by SQL readback. LR/RC timestamp regressions passed 35 tests across two files; the child UI suite passed five tests. Frontend publication, RC browser acceptance, complete closeout, revocation and second-device acceptance remain open.\\n\\nLatest root browser evidence: ordinary-account retained creation/claim/start opened the RC-first administrator QA run `qa-timed-rc-ui-cd812b72-4496-4738-bc7f-246cb4290279`. Next acknowledged question 2. Actual browser viewports at 390, 820, 1060, 1280 and 1600 pixels had document width equal to viewport for both LR and RC. Final production build subsequently passed; 755 canonical output files were checked. RC outage/reload, mandatory closeout, publication, revoked-write and second-device acceptance remain open. Configuration is enabled with the reviewed catalog and 30,000ms lease. It creates real parentless LR KV or RC session children. LR completion writes owned attempts, section history and mandatory wrong-answer journal rows in the same transaction. RC completion retains stable first-attempt UUIDs and adds explicit unanswered rows in that transaction. No PrepTest parent is fabricated.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-work-readiness.md:36:- 29 real PostgreSQL checks with separate ordinary login connections: identical create/close races, competing revisions, claim conflict, save-versus-revoke in both lock orderings, consistent read behind checkpoint, foreign denial and exact receipt replay against later authoritative state.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-work-readiness.md:43:Public transport: envelope `workKind: standalone-section`, UUID `workId`/`commandId`, `deviceId`, `expectedRevision`, `writerEpoch`, operation and payload. Create payload is `{kind, pt, section, pace}` with revision/epoch zero. `read_timed_section_v1` accepts `p_work_id`; create/commit accept `p_command`. Every response contains contract/kind, `serverTime`, authoritative `sitting`, native `child`, optional owner-bound `receipt` and `replayed`. Child is null before start; LR returns its PracticeSession record; RC returns session plus attempts. Checkpoint/close payloads retain the reviewed PT child shapes without weakening its PT parser. Error codes remain PT401/PT409/PT410/PT412/PT422/PT503.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-work-readiness.md:47:[The additive SQL artifact](artifacts/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-control-foundation.sql) creates a separate no-login/no-BYPASSRLS executor, disabled configuration, canonical frozen sitting identity, unique active source, append-only owner-bound receipts and source bindings. It references the already deployed catalog instead of copying the manifest or altering PT parents. Guards reject changed immutable identity, nonconsecutive revisions, decreasing elapsed time, device changes without epoch advancement, terminal rewrites, malformed clock/completion state and cross-kind bindings. Public capability always reports disabled and `foundation-only`; no public mutating RPC exists.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-work-readiness.md:49:[The isolated verifier](../../scripts/verification/standalone-timed-foundation.mjs) passed **31 assertions** on 2026-09-30 using in-memory PGlite with synthetic LR/RC catalog rows. Checks cover disabled capability, role privileges, preserved legacy profile values, owner/foreign reads, denied raw ordinary writes, exact canonical queue identity, active-source uniqueness and distinct retakes, immutable identity/terminal state, monotonic revisions/time/epochs, bindings and duplicate/foreign receipts. Its superuser session uses `SET ROLE` to simulate row policies; catalog membership is tested separately. This is not an ordinary JWT or PostgreSQL connection-race test.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-work-readiness.md:61:The smallest coherent expansion is a new account-owned sitting for one canonical LR or RC section selected from the already reviewed content manifest. They remain explicitly identified legacy paths until that work passes; this partial expansion cannot close #167.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-standalone-timed-work-readiness.md:76:| Receipts | Dedicated append-only section receipts keyed by owner/sitting/command UUID, storing canonical envelope and committed revision. Exact replay returns its receipt pl…1817 tokens truncated…y through the acknowledged lease expiry. Claim preserves saved answers and settles the clock before pausing and changing writer epoch. Checkpoint and heartbeat renew only an unexpired current writer. Break uses the same clock and heartbeat contract; early finish remains permitted.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-timed-pt-writer-readiness.md:21:RC checkpoints atomically save question position, clock, and immutable first-attempt identities. Frozen question-to-passage membership, owner/session attribution, selected choices, and types are validated. Normal answer revisits update the selected choice, correctness, and question time on the same attempt UUID; immutable command receipts preserve every acknowledged answer payload. A different first-attempt identity cannot replace that sitting\'s question row. Closeout grades current final choices, inserts null unanswered first-attempt rows, and commits the completed session and parent advancement together. RC elapsed time remains session clock time, not the sum of question times. The amended writer passed 160 isolated assertions, including stable-identity revisits and old-command replay returning current choices; the later amended suite passed 175 isolated assertions and the managed preservation harness passed seven checks including the metadata upgrade. These local checks remain distinct from live ordinary-account acceptance.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-timed-pt-writer-readiness.md:36:Success returns `{receipt,parent,checkpoint,serverTime,replayed}`. Receipt binds actor, work, UUID, operation, full canonical command, committed revision, and result. An identical retry acknowledges the original operation before current access/revision/lease rejection, while returning the latest parent and checkpoint. Changed payload rejects with `PT412`, distinct from a new-command `PT409` conflict. A replay never reapplies progress or restores an old parent. Revision/epoch/device/lease conflicts include current owner parent data in JSON error DETAIL.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-timed-pt-writer-readiness.md:46:The [fresh auth ACL evidence](../agent/verification/2026-09-30-timed-live-auth-grants.json) confirms managed postgres has auth schema USAGE without grant option. The foundation therefore does not try to grant auth schema access to the executor. A migration-owned, narrowly granted `public.timed_pt_actor_v1()` SECURITY DEFINER helper delegates to fully qualified `auth.uid()` under a fixed search path. It takes no actor argument and performs no writes. Only the executor can call it; public writer/read functions derive identity through that helper. Existing authenticated owner read policies retain their direct `auth.uid()` expression.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-timed-pt-writer-readiness.md:48:Fresh read-only evidence is recorded in [the live catalog](../agent/verification/2026-09-30-timed-live-catalog.json), [deployment role](../agent/verification/2026-09-30-timed-live-deployment-role.json), and [ownership catalog](../agent/verification/2026-09-30-timed-live-ownership.json). It confirms PostgreSQL 17.6, involved postgres-owned tables, relevant types/owner FKs, existing touch/profile triggers, and absent timed columns. The deployed class helper is absent; the draft does not require it. The managed query role is nonsuperuser with CREATEROLE and BYPASSRLS. Local equivalent-role validation is separate from an actual managed deployment.\\ndocs/implementation-plans/2026-09-30-timed-pt-writer-readiness.md:52:- [Isolated writer harness](../../scripts/verification/timed-pt-writer-sql.mjs), using [shared synthetic fixtures](../../scripts/verification/timed-pt-writer-fixtures.mjs), executes the SQL in external PGlite. Root re-enabled the same catalog and 30,000ms lease at 14:24 UTC. Actual LR browser verification then rendered the full question, acknowledged an answer and confirmed it by SQL readback. Blocking commit RPC hid questions and showed paused recovery; clearing the block and retrying the retained command recovered the question. Mandatory LR closeout was deliberately not exercised on this administrator fixture, to preserve its existing journal identities. LR/RC timestamp regressions passed 35 tests across two files; the child UI suite passed five tests. Reload preserved exactly 31:22 and the administrator fixture was left paused without mandatory closeout or journal mutation. The separate ordinary-tester baseline closeout probe passed; that is RPC evidence, not a browser closeout walk. All network blocks and device override were cleared. Final production build passed, checking 755 canonical output files without a canonical-content failure; 176 integrated tests across 15 files and typecheck passed. Frontend publication, mandatory closeout, revoked in-flight writes and actual second-device acceptance remain open.\\n"}'}]
